   701	
   702	    /// Returns a reference to the process status.
   703	    pub fn status(&self) -> &ProcessStatus {
   704	        &self.status
   705	    }
   706	
   707	    /// Stops the process.
   708	    //
   709	    // FIXME: `ptrace` is another reason that can cause a process to stop.
   710	    // Consider extending the method signature to support `ptrace` if necessary.
   711	    pub fn stop(&self, sig_num: SigNum) {
   712	        if self.status.stop_status().stop(sig_num) {
   713	            self.wake_up_parent();
   714	        }
   715	    }
   716	
   717	    /// Resumes the stopped process.
   718	    pub fn resume(&self) {
   719	        if self.status.stop_status().resume() {
   720	            self.wake_up_parent();
   721	
   722	            // Note that the resume function is called by the thread which deals with SIGCONT,
   723	            // since SIGCONT is handled by any thread in this process, we need to wake
   724	            // up other stopped threads in the same process.
   725	            for task in self.tasks.lock().as_slice() {
   726	                let posix_thread = task.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
   727	                posix_thread.wake_signalled_waker();
   728	            }
   729	        }
   730	    }
   731	
   732	    /// Returns whether the process is stopped.
   733	    pub fn is_stopped(&self) -> bool {
   734	        self.status.stop_status().is_stopped()
   735	    }
   736	
   737	    /// Gets and clears the stop status changes for the `wait` syscall.
   738	    pub(super) fn wait_stopped_or_continued(&self, options: WaitOptions) -> Option<StopWaitStatus> {
   739	        self.status.stop_status().wait(options)
   740	    }
   741	
   742	    fn wake_up_parent(&self) {
   743	        let parent_guard = self.parent.lock();
   744	        let parent = parent_guard.process().upgrade().unwrap();
   745	        parent.children_wait_queue.wake_all();
   746	    }
   747	
   748	    // ******************* Subreaper ********************
   749	
   750	    /// Sets the child subreaper attribute of the current process.
   751	    ///
   752	    /// # Panics
   753	    ///
   754	    /// This method may panic if the process is a zombie process.
   755	    pub fn set_child_subreaper(&self) {
   756	        self.is_child_subreaper.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   757	        let has_child_subreaper = self.has_child_subreaper.fetch_or(true, Ordering::Release);
   758	        if !has_child_subreaper {
   759	            self.propagate_has_child_subreaper();
   760	        }
   761	    }
   762	
   763	    /// Unsets the child subreaper attribute of the current process.
   764	    pub fn unset_child_subreaper(&self) {
   765	        self.is_child_subreaper.store(false, Ordering::Relaxed);
   766	    }
   767	
   768	    /// Returns whether this process is a child subreaper.
   769	    pub fn is_child_subreaper(&self) -> bool {
   770	        self.is_child_subreaper.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   771	    }
   772	
   773	    /// Sets all descendants of the current process as having child subreaper.
   774	    fn propagate_has_child_subreaper(&self) {
   775	        let mut process_queue = VecDeque::new();
   776	        let children = self.children().lock();
   777	        for child_process in children.as_ref().unwrap().values() {
   778	            let has_child_subreaper = child_process
   779	                .has_child_subreaper
   780	                .fetch_or(true, Ordering::Release);
   781	            if !has_child_subreaper {
   782	                process_queue.push_back(child_process.clone());
   783	            }
   784	        }
   785	
   786	        while let Some(process) = process_queue.pop_front() {
   787	            let children = process.children().lock();
   788	            let Some(children_ref) = children.as_ref() else {
   789	                // The process is exiting group at the same time.
   790	                continue;
   791	            };
   792	            for child_process in children_ref.values() {
   793	                let has_child_subreaper = child_process
   794	                    .has_child_subreaper
   795	                    .fetch_or(true, Ordering::Release);
   796	                if !has_child_subreaper {
   797	                    process_queue.push_back(child_process.clone());
   798	                }
   799	            }
   800	        }
   801	    }
   802	}

   813	        move || {
   814	            if let Some(process) = process.upgrade() {
   815	                process.enqueue_signal(KernelSignal::new(signum));

   702	#[expect(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
   703	fn create_child_process(
   704	    pid: Pid,
   705	    executable_path: &str,
   706	    process_vm: ProcessVm,
   707	    resource_limits: ResourceLimits,
   708	    nice: Nice,
   709	    oom_score_adj: i16,
   710	    sig_dispositions: Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>>,
   711	    user_ns: Arc<UserNamespace>,
   712	    thread_builder: PosixThreadBuilder,
   713	) -> Arc<Process> {
   714	    let child_proc = Process::new(
   715	        pid,
   716	        executable_path.to_string(),
   717	        process_vm,
   718	        resource_limits,
   719	        nice,
   720	        oom_score_adj,
   721	        sig_dispositions,
   722	        user_ns,
   723	    );
   724	
   725	    let child_task = thread_builder.process(Arc::downgrade(&child_proc)).build();
   726	    child_proc.tasks().lock().insert(child_task).unwrap();
   727	
   728	    child_proc
   729	}
   730	
   731	fn set_parent_and_group(clone_flags: CloneFlags, parent: &Arc<Process>, child: &Arc<Process>) {
   732	    loop {
   733	        let real_parent = clone_parent(clone_flags, parent);
   734	
   735	        // Lock the parent's children before checking its status.
   736	        let mut children_mut = real_parent.children().lock();
   737	
   738	        let Some(children_mut) = children_mut.as_mut() else {
   739	            // The real parent is concurrently exiting group.
   740	            // The children have been cleared,
   741	            // so the retrial will see an up-to-date real parent.
   742	            continue;
   743	        };
   744	
   745	        // Lock order: children of process -> parent of process
   746	        child.parent().lock().set_process(&real_parent);
   747	
   748	        // Update `has_child_subreaper` for the child process to
   749	        // make sure the `has_child_subreaper` state of the child process
   750	        // will be consistent with its parent.
   751	        if real_parent.has_child_subreaper.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
   752	            child.has_child_subreaper.store(true, Ordering::Release);
   753	        }
   754	
   755	        // Lock order: children of process -> process table
   756	        // -> group of process -> group inner
   757	
   758	        let mut process_table_mut = process_table::process_table_mut();
   759	
   760	        let process_group_mut = parent.process_group.lock();
   761	
   762	        let process_group = process_group_mut.upgrade().unwrap();
   763	        let mut process_group_inner = process_group.lock();
   764	
   765	        // Put the child process in the parent's process group
   766	        process_group_inner.insert_process(child.clone());
   767	        *child.process_group.lock() = Arc::downgrade(&process_group);
   768	
   769	        // Put the child process in the parent's `children` field
   770	        children_mut.insert(child.pid(), child.clone());
   771	
   772	        // Put the child process in the global table
   773	        process_table_mut.insert(child.pid(), child.clone());
   774	
   775	        return;
   776	    }
   777	}
   778	
   779	fn clone_parent(clone_flags: CloneFlags, current: &Arc<Process>) -> Cow<'_, Arc<Process>> {
   780	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT) {

    20	// The definition of WaitOptions is from Occlum
    21	bitflags! {
    22	    pub struct WaitOptions: u32 {
    23	        const WNOHANG = 0x1;
    24	        const WSTOPPED = 0x2; // Same as WUNTRACED
    25	        const WEXITED = 0x4;
    26	        const WCONTINUED = 0x8;
    27	        const WNOWAIT = 0x01000000;
    28	        const WNOTHREAD = 0x20000000;
    29	        const WALL = 0x40000000;
    30	        const WCLONE = 0x80000000;
    31	    }
    32	}
    33	
    34	impl WaitOptions {
    35	    pub fn check(&self) -> Result<()> {
    36	        // FIXME: The syscall `waitid` allows using WNOWAIT with
    37	        // WSTOPPED or WCONTINUED
    38	        if self.intersects(WaitOptions::WSTOPPED | WaitOptions::WCONTINUED)
    39	            && self.contains(WaitOptions::WNOWAIT)
    40	        {
    41	            return_errno_with_message!(
    42	                Errno::EINVAL,
    43	                "WNOWAIT cannot be used toghther with WSTOPPED or WCONTINUED"
    44	            );
    45	        }
    46	
    47	        let supported_args = WaitOptions::WNOHANG
    48	            | WaitOptions::WSTOPPED
    49	            | WaitOptions::WCONTINUED
    50	            | WaitOptions::WNOWAIT;
    51	        if !supported_args.contains(*self) {
    52	            warn!(
    53	                "unsupported wait options are found: {:?}",
    54	                *self - supported_args
    55	            );
    56	        }
    57	
    58	        Ok(())
    59	    }
    60	}
    61	
    62	pub fn do_wait(
    63	    child_filter: ProcessFilter,
    64	    wait_options: WaitOptions,
    65	    ctx: &Context,
    66	) -> Result<Option<WaitStatus>> {
    67	    wait_options.check()?;
    68	
    69	    let is_nonblocking = if let ProcessFilter::WithPidfd(pid_file) = &child_filter {
    70	        pid_file.is_nonblocking()
    71	    } else {
    72	        false
    73	    };
    74	
    75	    let zombie_child = with_sigmask_changed(
    76	        ctx,
    77	        |sigmask| sigmask + SIGCHLD,
    78	        || {
    79	            ctx.process.children_wait_queue().pause_until(|| {
    80	                // Acquire the children lock at first to prevent race conditions.
    81	                // We want to ensure that multiple waiting threads
    82	                // do not return the same waited process status.
    83	                let mut children_lock = ctx.process.children().lock();
    84	                let children_mut = children_lock.as_mut().unwrap();
    85	
    86	                let unwaited_children = children_mut
    87	                    .values()
    88	                    .filter(|child| match &child_filter {
    89	                        ProcessFilter::Any => true,
    90	                        ProcessFilter::WithPid(pid) => child.pid() == *pid,
    91	                        ProcessFilter::WithPgid(pgid) => child.pgid() == *pgid,
    92	                        ProcessFilter::WithPidfd(pid_file) => {
    93	                            Arc::ptr_eq(pid_file.process(), *child)
    94	                        }
    95	                    })
    96	                    .collect::<Box<_>>();
    97	
    98	                if unwaited_children.is_empty() {
    99	                    return Some(Err(Error::with_message(
   100	                        Errno::ECHILD,
   101	                        "the process has no child to wait",
   102	                    )));
   103	                }
   104	
   105	                if let Some(status) = wait_zombie(&unwaited_children) {
   106	                    if !wait_options.contains(WaitOptions::WNOWAIT) {
   107	                        reap_zombie_child(
   108	                            status.pid(),
   109	                            children_mut,
   110	                            ctx.process.reaped_children_stats(),
   111	                        );
   112	                    }
   113	                    return Some(Ok(Some(status)));
   114	                }
   115	
   116	                if let Some(status) = wait_stopped_or_continued(&unwaited_children, wait_options) {
   117	                    return Some(Ok(Some(status)));
   118	                }
   119	
   120	                if wait_options.contains(WaitOptions::WNOHANG) {
   121	                    return Some(Ok(None));
   122	                }
   123	
   124	                if is_nonblocking {
   125	                    return Some(Err(Error::with_message(
   126	                        Errno::EAGAIN,
   127	                        "the PID file is nonblocking and the child has not terminated",
   128	                    )));
   129	                }
   130	
   131	                // wait
   132	                None
   133	            })
   134	        },
   135	    )??;
   136	
   137	    Ok(zombie_child)
   138	}
   139	
   140	pub enum WaitStatus {
   141	    Zombie(Arc<Process>),
   142	    Stop(Arc<Process>, SigNum),
   143	    Continue(Arc<Process>),
   144	}
   145	
   146	impl WaitStatus {
   147	    pub fn pid(&self) -> Pid {
   148	        self.process().pid()
   149	    }

     3	//! Scheduling related information in a task.
     4	
     5	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
     6	
     7	use crate::{cpu::CpuId, task::Task};
     8	
     9	/// Fields of a task that OSTD will never touch.
    10	///
    11	/// The type ought to be defined by the OSTD user and injected into the task.
    12	/// They are not part of the dynamic task data because it's slower there for
    13	/// the user-defined scheduler to access. The better ways to let the user
    14	/// define them, such as
    15	/// [existential types](https://github.com/rust-lang/rfcs/pull/2492) do not
    16	/// exist yet. So we decide to define them in OSTD.
    17	#[derive(Debug)]
    18	pub struct TaskScheduleInfo {
    19	    /// The CPU that the task would like to be running on.
    20	    pub cpu: AtomicCpuId,
    21	}
    22	
    23	/// An atomic CPUID container.
    24	#[derive(Debug)]
    25	pub struct AtomicCpuId(AtomicU32);
    26	
    27	impl AtomicCpuId {
    28	    /// The null value of CPUID.
    29	    ///
    30	    /// An `AtomicCpuId` with `AtomicCpuId::NONE` as its inner value is empty.
    31	    const NONE: u32 = u32::MAX;
    32	
    33	    /// Sets the inner value of an `AtomicCpuId` if it's empty.
    34	    ///
    35	    /// The return value is a result indicating whether the new value was written
    36	    /// and containing the previous value. If the previous value is empty, it returns
    37	    /// `Ok(())`. Otherwise, it returns `Err(previous_value)` which the previous
    38	    /// value is a valid CPU ID.
    39	    pub fn set_if_is_none(&self, cpu_id: CpuId) -> core::result::Result<(), CpuId> {
    40	        self.0
    41	            .compare_exchange(
    42	                Self::NONE,
    43	                cpu_id.as_usize() as u32,
    44	                Ordering::Relaxed,
    45	                Ordering::Relaxed,
    46	            )
    47	            .map(|_| ())
    48	            .map_err(|prev| (prev as usize).try_into().unwrap())
    49	    }
    50	
    51	    /// Sets the inner value of an `AtomicCpuId` anyway.
    52	    pub fn set_anyway(&self, cpu_id: CpuId) {
    53	        self.0.store(cpu_id.as_usize() as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);
    54	    }
    55	
    56	    /// Sets the inner value of an `AtomicCpuId` to `AtomicCpuId::NONE`, i.e. makes
    57	    /// an `AtomicCpuId` empty.
    58	    pub fn set_to_none(&self) {
    59	        self.0.store(Self::NONE, Ordering::Relaxed);
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    /// Gets the inner value of an `AtomicCpuId`.
    63	    pub fn get(&self) -> Option<CpuId> {
    64	        let val = self.0.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    65	        if val == Self::NONE {
    66	            None
    67	        } else {
    68	            Some((val as usize).try_into().ok()?)
    69	        }
    70	    }
    71	}
    72	
    73	impl Default for AtomicCpuId {
    74	    fn default() -> Self {
    75	        Self(AtomicU32::new(Self::NONE))
    76	    }
    77	}

    21	/// Exits the current POSIX thread.
    22	///
    23	/// # Panics
    24	///
    25	/// If the current thread is not a POSIX thread, this method will panic.
    26	pub fn do_exit(term_status: TermStatus) {
    27	    exit_internal(term_status, false);
    28	}
    29	
    30	/// Kills all threads and exits the current POSIX process.
    31	///
    32	/// # Panics
    33	///
    34	/// If the current thread is not a POSIX thread, this method will panic.
    35	pub fn do_exit_group(term_status: TermStatus) {
    36	    exit_internal(term_status, true);
    37	}
    38	
    39	/// Exits the current POSIX thread or process.
    40	fn exit_internal(term_status: TermStatus, is_exiting_group: bool) {
    41	    let current_task = Task::current().unwrap();
    42	    let current_thread = current_task.as_thread().unwrap();
    43	    let posix_thread = current_thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
    44	    let thread_local = current_task.as_thread_local().unwrap();
    45	    let posix_process = posix_thread.process();
    46	
    47	    let is_last_thread = {
    48	        let mut tasks = posix_process.tasks().lock();
    49	        let has_exited_group = tasks.has_exited_group();
    50	
    51	        if is_exiting_group && !has_exited_group {
    52	            sigkill_other_threads(&current_task, &tasks);
    53	            tasks.set_exited_group();
    54	        }
    55	
    56	        // According to Linux's behavior, the last thread's exit code will become the process's
    57	        // exit code, so here we should just overwrite the old value (if any).
    58	        if !has_exited_group {
    59	            posix_process.status().set_exit_code(term_status.as_u32());
    60	        }
    61	
    62	        // We should only change the thread status when running as the thread, so no race
    63	        // conditions can occur in between.
    64	        if current_thread.is_exited() {
    65	            return;
    66	        }
    67	        current_thread.exit();
    68	
    69	        tasks.remove_exited(&current_task)
    70	    };
    71	
    72	    wake_clear_ctid(thread_local);
    73	
    74	    wake_robust_list(thread_local, posix_thread.tid());
    75	
    76	    // According to Linux behavior, the main thread shouldn't be removed from the table until the
    77	    // process is reaped by its parent.
    78	    if posix_thread.tid() != posix_process.pid() {
    79	        thread_table::remove_thread(posix_thread.tid());
    80	    }
    81	
    82	    // Drop fields in `PosixThread`.
    83	    *posix_thread.file_table().lock() = None;
    84	    *posix_thread.ns_proxy().lock() = None;
    85	
    86	    // Drop fields in `ThreadLocal`.
    87	    *thread_local.root_vmar().borrow_mut() = None;
    88	    thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut().remove();
    89	    thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy_mut().remove();
    90	
    91	    if is_last_thread {
    92	        exit_process(&posix_process);
    93	    }
    94	}
    95	
    96	/// Sends `SIGKILL` to all other threads in the current process.
    97	///
    98	/// This is only needed when initiating an `exit_group` for the first time.
    99	fn sigkill_other_threads(current_task: &CurrentTask, task_set: &TaskSet) {
   100	    for task in task_set.as_slice() {
   101	        if core::ptr::eq(current_task.as_ref(), task.as_ref()) {
   102	            continue;
   103	        }
   104	        task.as_posix_thread()
   105	            .unwrap()
   106	            .enqueue_signal(Box::new(KernelSignal::new(SIGKILL)));
   107	    }
   108	}
   109	
   110	/// Writes zero to `clear_child_tid` and performs a futex wake.
   111	fn wake_clear_ctid(thread_local: &ThreadLocal) {
   112	    let clear_ctid = thread_local.clear_child_tid().get();
   113	
   114	    if clear_ctid == 0 {
   115	        return;
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    let _ = current_userspace!()
   119	        .write_val(clear_ctid, &0u32)
   120	        .inspect_err(|err| debug!("exit: cannot clear the child TID: {:?}", err));
   121	    let _ = futex_wake(clear_ctid, 1, None)
   122	        .inspect_err(|err| debug!("exit: cannot wake the futex on the child TID: {:?}", err));
   123	
   124	    thread_local.clear_child_tid().set(0);
   125	}
   126	
   127	/// Walks the robust futex list, marking futex dead and waking waiters.
   128	///
   129	/// This corresponds to Linux's `exit_robust_list`. Errors are silently ignored.
   130	fn wake_robust_list(thread_local: &ThreadLocal, tid: Tid) {
   131	    let mut robust_list = thread_local.robust_list().borrow_mut();
   132	
   133	    let list_head = match *robust_list {
   134	        Some(robust_list_head) => robust_list_head,
   135	        None => return,
   136	    };
   137	
   138	    trace!("exit: wake up the rubust list: {:?}", list_head);
   139	    for futex_addr in list_head.futexes() {
   140	        let _ = wake_robust_futex(futex_addr, tid)
   141	            .inspect_err(|err| debug!("exit: cannot wake up the robust futex: {:?}", err));
   142	    }
   143	
   144	    *robust_list = None;
   145	}

     3	//! The process status.
     4	
     5	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicU32, Ordering};
     6	
     7	use ostd::sync::SpinLock;
     8	
     9	use super::ExitCode;
    10	use crate::process::{signal::sig_num::SigNum, WaitOptions};
    11	
    12	/// The status of a process.
    13	///
    14	/// This maintains:
    15	/// 1. Whether the process is a zombie (i.e., all its threads have exited);
    16	/// 2. Whether the process is the vfork child, which shares the user-space virtual memory
    17	///    with its parent process;
    18	/// 3. The exit code of the process;
    19	/// 4. Whether the process is stopped (by a signal or ptrace).
    20	#[derive(Debug)]
    21	pub struct ProcessStatus {
    22	    is_zombie: AtomicBool,
    23	    is_vfork_child: AtomicBool,
    24	    exit_code: AtomicU32,
    25	    stop_status: StopStatus,
    26	}
    27	
    28	impl Default for ProcessStatus {
    29	    fn default() -> Self {
    30	        Self {
    31	            is_zombie: AtomicBool::new(false),
    32	            is_vfork_child: AtomicBool::new(false),
    33	            exit_code: AtomicU32::new(0),
    34	            stop_status: StopStatus::new(),
    35	        }
    36	    }
    37	}
    38	
    39	impl ProcessStatus {
    40	    /// Returns whether the process is a zombie process.
    41	    pub fn is_zombie(&self) -> bool {
    42	        // Use the `Acquire` memory order to make the exit code visible.
    43	        self.is_zombie.load(Ordering::Acquire)
    44	    }
    45	
    46	    /// Sets the process to be a zombie process.
    47	    ///
    48	    /// This method should be called when the process completes its exit. The current thread must
    49	    /// be the last thread in the process, so that no threads belonging to the process can run
    50	    /// after it.
    51	    pub(super) fn set_zombie(&self) {
    52	        // Use the `Release` memory order to make the exit code visible.
    53	        self.is_zombie.store(true, Ordering::Release);
    54	    }
    55	}
    56	
    57	impl ProcessStatus {
    58	    /// Returns whether the process is the vfork child.
    59	    pub fn is_vfork_child(&self) -> bool {
    60	        self.is_vfork_child.load(Ordering::Acquire)
    61	    }
    62	
    63	    /// Sets whether the process is the vfork child.
    64	    pub fn set_vfork_child(&self, is_vfork_child: bool) {
    65	        self.is_vfork_child.store(is_vfork_child, Ordering::Release);
    66	    }
    67	}
    68	
    69	impl ProcessStatus {
    70	    /// Returns the exit code.
    71	    pub fn exit_code(&self) -> ExitCode {
    72	        self.exit_code.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
    73	    }
    74	
    75	    /// Sets the exit code.
    76	    pub(super) fn set_exit_code(&self, exit_code: ExitCode) {
    77	        self.exit_code.store(exit_code, Ordering::Relaxed);
    78	    }
    79	}
    80	
    81	impl ProcessStatus {
    82	    pub(super) fn stop_status(&self) -> &StopStatus {
    83	        &self.stop_status
    84	    }
    85	}
    86	
    87	#[derive(Debug)]
    88	pub(super) struct StopStatus {
    89	    /// Indicates whether the process is stopped.
    90	    is_stopped: AtomicBool,
    91	
    92	    /// Indicates whether the process's status has changed and has not yet been waited on.
    93	    ///
    94	    /// User programs may use the wait* syscalls to check for changes in
    95	    /// the process's status. This field will be set to `Some(_)` once the
    96	    /// process's status changes and will be set to `None` if the process
    97	    /// has already been waited on.
    98	    wait_status: SpinLock<Option<StopWaitStatus>>,
    99	}

   632	        &self.sig_dispositions
   633	    }
   634	
   635	    /// Enqueues a process-directed signal.
   636	    ///
   637	    /// This method should only be used for enqueue kernel signals and fault signals.
   638	    ///
   639	    /// The signal may be delivered to any one of the threads that does not currently have the
   640	    /// signal blocked. If more than one of the threads have the signal unblocked, then this method
   641	    /// chooses an arbitrary thread to which to deliver the signal.
   642	    //
   643	    // TODO: Restrict this method with the access control tool.
   644	    pub fn enqueue_signal(&self, signal: impl Signal + Clone + 'static) {
   645	        if self.status.is_zombie() {
   646	            return;
   647	        }
   648	
   649	        let sig_dispositions = self.sig_dispositions.lock();
   650	
   651	        // Drop the signal if it's ignored. See explanation at `enqueue_signal_locked`.
   652	        let signum = signal.num();
   653	        if sig_dispositions.get(signum).will_ignore(signum) {
   654	            return;
   655	        }
   656	
   657	        let threads = self.tasks.lock();
   658	
   659	        // Enqueue the signal to the first thread that does not block the signal.
   660	        for thread in threads.as_slice() {
   661	            let posix_thread = thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
   662	            if !posix_thread.has_signal_blocked(signal.num()) {
   663	                posix_thread.enqueue_signal_locked(Box::new(signal), sig_dispositions);
   664	                return;
   665	            }
   666	        }
   667	
   668	        // If all threads block the signal, enqueue the signal to the main thread.
   669	        let thread = threads.main();
   670	        let posix_thread = thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap();
   671	        posix_thread.enqueue_signal_locked(Box::new(signal), sig_dispositions);
   672	    }
   673

   193	        assert!(signalled_waker.is_none());
   194	        *signalled_waker = Some(waker);
   195	    }

   198	    pub fn clear_signalled_waker(&self) {
   199	        *self.signalled_waker.lock() = None;
   200	    }
   201	
   202	    /// Wakes up the signalled waker.
   203	    pub fn wake_signalled_waker(&self) {
   204	        if let Some(waker) = &*self.signalled_waker.lock() {
   205	            waker.wake_up();
   206	        }
   207	    }
   208	
   209	    /// Enqueues a thread-directed signal.
   210	    ///
   211	    /// This method does not perform permission checks on user signals. Therefore, unless the
   212	    /// caller can ensure that there are no permission issues, this method should be used for
   213	    /// enqueue kernel signals or fault signals.
   214	    pub fn enqueue_signal(&self, signal: Box<dyn Signal>) {
   215	        let process = self.process();
   216	        let sig_dispositions = process.sig_dispositions().lock();
   217	
   218	        let signum = signal.num();
   219	        if sig_dispositions.get(signum).will_ignore(signum) {
   220	            return;
   221	        }
   222	
   223	        self.enqueue_signal_locked(signal, sig_dispositions);
   224	    }
   225	
   226	    /// Enqueues a thread-directed signal with locked dispositions.
   227	    ///
   228	    /// By locking dispositions, the caller should have already checked the signal is not to be
   229	    /// ignored.
   230	    //
   231	    // FIXME: According to Linux behavior, we should enqueue ignored signals blocked by all
   232	    // threads, as a thread may change the signal handler and unblock them in the future. However,
   233	    // achieving this behavior properly without maintaining a process-wide signal queue is
   234	    // difficult. For instance, if we randomly select a thread-wide signal queue, the thread that
   235	    // modifies the signal handler and unblocks the signal may not be the same one. Consequently,
   236	    // the current implementation uses a simpler mechanism that never enqueues any ignored signals.
   237	    pub(in crate::process) fn enqueue_signal_locked(
   238	        &self,
   239	        signal: Box<dyn Signal>,
   240	        _sig_dispositions: MutexGuard<SigDispositions>,
   241	    ) {
   242	        self.sig_queues.enqueue(signal);
   243	        self.wake_signalled_waker();
   244	    }

   180	fn wait_stopped_or_continued(
   181	    unwaited_children: &[&Arc<Process>],
   182	    wait_options: WaitOptions,
   183	) -> Option<WaitStatus> {
   184	    if !wait_options.intersects(WaitOptions::WSTOPPED | WaitOptions::WCONTINUED) {
   185	        return None;
   186	    }
   187	
   188	    // Lock order: children of process -> tasks of process
   189	    for process in unwaited_children.iter() {
   190	        let Some(stop_wait_status) = process.wait_stopped_or_continued(wait_options) else {
   191	            continue;
   192	        };
   193	
   194	        let wait_status = match stop_wait_status {
   195	            StopWaitStatus::Stopped(sig_num) => WaitStatus::Stop((*process).clone(), sig_num),
   196	            StopWaitStatus::Continue => WaitStatus::Continue((*process).clone()),
   197	        };
   198	        return Some(wait_status);
   199	    }
   200	
   201	    None
   202	}
   203	
   204	/// Free zombie child with `child_pid`, returns the exit code of child process.
   205	fn reap_zombie_child(
   206	    child_pid: Pid,
   207	    children_lock: &mut BTreeMap<Pid, Arc<Process>>,
   208	    reaped_children_stats: &Mutex<ReapedChildrenStats>,
   209	) -> ExitCode {
   210	    let child_process = children_lock.remove(&child_pid).unwrap();
   211	    assert!(child_process.status().is_zombie());
   212	
   213	    for task in child_process.tasks().lock().as_slice() {
   214	        thread_table::remove_thread(task.as_posix_thread().unwrap().tid());
   215	    }
   216	
   217	    // Lock order: children of process -> session table -> group table
   218	    // -> process table -> group of process -> group inner -> session inner
   219	    let mut session_table_mut = process_table::session_table_mut();
   220	    let mut group_table_mut = process_table::group_table_mut();
   221	
   222	    // Remove the process from the global table
   223	    let mut process_table_mut = process_table::process_table_mut();
   224	    process_table_mut.remove(child_process.pid());
   225	
   226	    // Remove the process group and the session from global table, if necessary
   227	    let mut child_group_mut = child_process.process_group.lock();
   228	    child_process.clear_old_group_and_session(
   229	        &mut child_group_mut,
   230	        &mut session_table_mut,
   231	        &mut group_table_mut,
   232	    );
   233	    *child_group_mut = Weak::new();
   234	
   235	    let (mut user_time, mut kernel_time) = child_process.reaped_children_stats().lock().get();
   236	    user_time += child_process.prof_clock().user_clock().read_time();
   237	    kernel_time += child_process.prof_clock().kernel_clock().read_time();
   238	    reaped_children_stats.lock().add(user_time, kernel_time);
   239	
   240	    child_process.status().exit_code()
   241	}

    37	bitflags! {
    38	    #[derive(Default)]
    39	    pub struct CloneFlags: u32 {
    40	        const CLONE_NEWTIME = 0x00000080;       /* New time namespace */
    41	        const CLONE_VM      = 0x00000100;       /* Set if VM shared between processes.  */
    42	        const CLONE_FS      = 0x00000200;       /* Set if fs info shared between processes.  */
    43	        const CLONE_FILES   = 0x00000400;       /* Set if open files shared between processes.  */
    44	        const CLONE_SIGHAND = 0x00000800;       /* Set if signal handlers shared.  */
    45	        const CLONE_PIDFD   = 0x00001000;       /* Set if a pidfd should be placed in parent.  */
    46	        const CLONE_PTRACE  = 0x00002000;       /* Set if tracing continues on the child.  */
    47	        const CLONE_VFORK   = 0x00004000;       /* Set if the parent wants the child to wake it up on mm_release.  */
    48	        const CLONE_PARENT  = 0x00008000;       /* Set if we want to have the same parent as the cloner.  */
    49	        const CLONE_THREAD  = 0x00010000;       /* Set to add to same thread group.  */
    50	        const CLONE_NEWNS   = 0x00020000;       /* Set to create new namespace.  */
    51	        const CLONE_SYSVSEM = 0x00040000;       /* Set to shared SVID SEM_UNDO semantics.  */
    52	        const CLONE_SETTLS  = 0x00080000;       /* Set TLS info.  */
    53	        const CLONE_PARENT_SETTID = 0x00100000; /* Store TID in userlevel buffer before MM copy.  */
    54	        const CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID = 0x00200000;/* Register exit futex and memory location to clear.  */
    55	        const CLONE_DETACHED = 0x00400000;      /* Create clone detached.  */
    56	        const CLONE_UNTRACED = 0x00800000;      /* Set if the tracing process can't force CLONE_PTRACE on this clone.  */
    57	        const CLONE_CHILD_SETTID = 0x01000000;  /* Store TID in userlevel buffer in the child.  */
    58	        const CLONE_NEWCGROUP   = 0x02000000;	/* New cgroup namespace.  */
    59	        const CLONE_NEWUTS	= 0x04000000;	    /* New utsname group.  */
    60	        const CLONE_NEWIPC	= 0x08000000;	    /* New ipcs.  */
    61	        const CLONE_NEWUSER	= 0x10000000;	    /* New user namespace.  */
    62	        const CLONE_NEWPID	= 0x20000000;	    /* New pid namespace.  */
    63	        const CLONE_NEWNET	= 0x40000000;	    /* New network namespace.  */
    64	        const CLONE_IO	= 0x80000000;	        /* Clone I/O context.  */
    65	
    66	        /// A bitmask of all `CloneFlags` related to namespace creation.
    67	        const CLONE_NS_FLAGS = Self::CLONE_NEWTIME.bits() |
    68	            Self::CLONE_NEWNS.bits() |
    69	            Self::CLONE_NEWCGROUP.bits() |
    70	            Self::CLONE_NEWUTS.bits() |
    71	            Self::CLONE_NEWIPC.bits() |
    72	            Self::CLONE_NEWUSER.bits() |
    73	            Self::CLONE_NEWPID.bits() |
    74	            Self::CLONE_NEWNET.bits();
    75	    }
    76	}

    79	    FORKS_COUNTER.call_once(PerCpuCounter::new);

    82	/// An internal structure to homogenize the arguments for `clone` and
    83	/// `clone3`.
    84	///
    85	/// From the clone(2) man page:
    86	///
    87	/// ```
    88	/// The following table shows the equivalence between the arguments
    89	/// of clone() and the fields in the clone_args argument supplied to
    90	/// clone3():
    91	///     clone()         clone3()        Notes
    92	///                     cl_args field
    93	///     flags & ~0xff   flags           For most flags; details
    94	///                                     below
    95	///     parent_tid      pidfd           See CLONE_PIDFD
    96	///     child_tid       child_tid       See CLONE_CHILD_SETTID
    97	///     parent_tid      parent_tid      See CLONE_PARENT_SETTID
    98	///     flags & 0xff    exit_signal
    99	///     stack           stack
   100	///     ---             stack_size
   101	///     tls             tls             See CLONE_SETTLS
   102	///     ---             set_tid         See below for details
   103	///     ---             set_tid_size
   104	///     ---             cgroup          See CLONE_INTO_CGROUP
   105	/// ```
   106	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
   107	pub struct CloneArgs {
   108	    pub flags: CloneFlags,
   109	    pub pidfd: Option<Vaddr>,
   110	    pub child_tid: Vaddr,
   111	    pub parent_tid: Option<Vaddr>,
   112	    pub exit_signal: Option<SigNum>,
   113	    pub stack: Option<NonZeroU64>,
   114	    pub stack_size: Option<NonZeroU64>,
   115	    pub tls: u64,
   116	    pub _set_tid: Option<u64>,
   117	    pub _set_tid_size: Option<u64>,
   118	    pub _cgroup: Option<u64>,
   119	}
   120	
   121	impl CloneArgs {
   122	    /// Prepares a new [`CloneArgs`] based on the arguments for clone(2).
   123	    pub fn for_clone(
   124	        raw_flags: u64,
   125	        parent_tid: Vaddr,
   126	        child_tid: Vaddr,
   127	        tls: u64,
   128	        stack: u64,
   129	    ) -> Result<Self> {
   130	        const FLAG_MASK: u64 = 0xff;
   131	        let flags = CloneFlags::from(raw_flags & !FLAG_MASK);
   132	        let exit_signal = raw_flags & FLAG_MASK;
   133	
   134	        // Disambiguate the `parent_tid` parameter. The field is used
   135	        // both for `CLONE_PIDFD` and `CLONE_PARENT_SETTID`, so at
   136	        // most only one can be specified.
   137	        let (pidfd, parent_tid) = match (
   138	            flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD),
   139	            flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT_SETTID),
   140	        ) {
   141	            (false, false) => (None, None),
   142	            (true, false) => (Some(parent_tid), None),
   143	            (false, true) => (None, Some(parent_tid)),
   144	            (true, true) => {
   145	                return_errno_with_message!(
   146	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   147	                    "`CLONE_PIDFD` and `CLONE_PARENT_SETTID` cannot be specified together"
   148	                );
   149	            }
   150	        };
   151	
   152	        Ok(Self {
   153	            flags,
   154	            pidfd,
   155	            child_tid,
   156	            parent_tid,
   157	            exit_signal: (exit_signal != 0).then(|| SigNum::from_u8(exit_signal as u8)),
   158	            stack: NonZeroU64::new(stack),
   159	            tls,
   160	            ..Default::default()
   161	        })
   162	    }
   163	
   164	    pub fn for_fork() -> Self {
   165	        Self {
   166	            exit_signal: Some(SIGCHLD),
   167	            ..Default::default()
   168	        }
   169	    }
   170	
   171	    pub fn for_vfork() -> Self {
   172	        Self {
   173	            flags: CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK | CloneFlags::CLONE_VM,
   174	            exit_signal: Some(SIGCHLD),
   175	            ..Default::default()
   176	        }
   177	    }
   178	
   179	    pub(self) fn check(&self, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
   180	        let clone_flags = self.flags;
   560	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   561	) -> Result<()> {
   562	    if let Some(addr) =
   563	        parent_tidptr.filter(|_| clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT_SETTID))
   564	    {
   565	        current_userspace!().write_val(addr, &child_tid)?;
   566	    }
   567	    Ok(())
   568	}
   569	
   570	fn clone_vm(parent_process_vm: &ProcessVm, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> Result<ProcessVm> {
   571	    // If CLONE_VM is set, the child and parent share the same VMAR.
   572	    // Otherwise, the child has a copy of the parent's VMAR.
   573	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VM) {
   574	        Ok(parent_process_vm.clone())
   575	    } else {
   576	        ProcessVm::fork_from(parent_process_vm)
   577	    }
   578	}
   579	
   580	fn clone_user_ctx(
   581	    parent_context: &UserContext,
   582	    new_sp: Option<NonZeroU64>,
   583	    stack_size: Option<NonZeroU64>,
   584	    tls: u64,
   585	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   586	) -> UserContext {
   587	    let mut child_context = parent_context.clone();
   588	    // The return value in the child thread is zero.
   589	    child_context.set_syscall_ret(0);
   590	
   591	    if let Some(new_sp) = new_sp {
   592	        if let Some(stack_size) = stack_size {
   593	            // `new_sp` is the stack bottom if the stack size is specified.
   594	            child_context.set_stack_pointer((new_sp.get() + stack_size.get()) as usize);
   595	        } else {
   596	            // `new_sp` is the stack top if the stack size is not specified.
   597	            child_context.set_stack_pointer(new_sp.get() as usize);
   598	        }
   599	    }
   600	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SETTLS) {
   601	        child_context.set_tls_pointer(tls as usize);
   602	    }
   603	
   604	    child_context
   605	}
   606	
   607	fn clone_fs(parent_fs: &Arc<ThreadFsInfo>, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> Arc<ThreadFsInfo> {
   608	    // If CLONE_FS is set, the child and parent share the same filesystem information.
   609	    // Otherwise, the child has a copy of the parent's filesystem information.
   610	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FS) {
   611	        parent_fs.clone()
   612	    } else {
   613	        Arc::new(parent_fs.as_ref().clone())
   614	    }
   615	}
   616	
   617	fn clone_files(parent_file_table: &RwArc<FileTable>, clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> RwArc<FileTable> {
   618	    // If CLONE_FILES is set, the child and parent share the same file table.
   619	    // Otherwise, the child has a copy of the parent's file table.
   620	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FILES) {
   621	        parent_file_table.clone()
   622	    } else {
   623	        RwArc::new(parent_file_table.read().clone())
   624	    }
   625	}
   626	
   627	fn clone_sighand(
   628	    parent_sig_dispositions: &Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>>,
   629	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   630	) -> Arc<Mutex<SigDispositions>> {
   631	    // If CLONE_SIGHAND is set, the child and parent shares the same signal handlers.
   632	    // Otherwise, the child has a copy of the parent's signal handlers.
   633	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND) {
   634	        parent_sig_dispositions.clone()
   635	    } else {
   636	        Arc::new(Mutex::new(*parent_sig_dispositions.lock()))
   637	    }
   638	}
   639	
   640	fn clone_sysvsem(clone_flags: CloneFlags) -> Result<()> {
   641	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SYSVSEM) {
   642	        warn!("CLONE_SYSVSEM is not supported now");
   643	    }
   644	    Ok(())
   645	}
   646	
   647	fn clone_pidfd(
   648	    ctx: &Context,
   649	    child: &Arc<Process>,
   650	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   651	    pidfd_addr: Option<Vaddr>,
   652	) -> Result<()> {
   653	    if !clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD) {
   654	        return Ok(());
   655	    }
   656	
   657	    let pidfd_addr = pidfd_addr.unwrap();
   658	
   659	    let fd = {
   660	        let pid_file = PidFile::new(child.clone(), false);
   661	        let file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table();
   662	        let mut file_table_locked = file_table.unwrap().write();
   663	        file_table_locked.insert(Arc::new(pid_file), FdFlags::CLOEXEC)
   664	    };
   665	
   666	    // Since `write_val` may sleep, we cannot hold the file table lock during its execution.
   667	    // FIXME: Should we remove the file from the file table if the write operation fails?
   668	    match ctx.user_space().write_val(pidfd_addr, &fd) {
   669	        Ok(()) => Ok(()),
   670	        Err(e) => {
   671	            let file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table();
   672	            let mut file_table_locked = file_table.unwrap().write();
   673	            file_table_locked.close_file(fd);
   674	            Err(e)
   675	        }
   676	    }
   677	}
   678	
   679	fn clone_user_ns(
   680	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   681	    thread_local: &ThreadLocal,
   682	) -> Result<Arc<UserNamespace>> {
   683	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWUSER) {
   684	        return_errno_with_message!(
   685	            Errno::EINVAL,
   686	            "cloning a new user namespace is not supported"
   687	        );
   688	    } else {
   689	        Ok(thread_local.borrow_user_ns().clone())
   690	    }
   691	}
   692	
   693	fn clone_ns_proxy(
   694	    parent_ns_proxy: &Arc<NsProxy>,
   695	    user_ns: &Arc<UserNamespace>,
   696	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   697	    posix_thread: &PosixThread,
   698	) -> Result<Arc<NsProxy>> {
   699	    parent_ns_proxy.new_clone(user_ns, clone_flags, posix_thread)
   700	}

   178	
   179	    pub(self) fn check(&self, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
   180	        let clone_flags = self.flags;
   181	        clone_flags.check_unsupported_flags()?;
   182	
   183	        // This checks the arguments for all clone-related system calls.
   184	        // Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.16.9/source/kernel/fork.c#L1926-L1978>.
   185	
   186	        // Reject invalid argument combinations related to the CLONE_FS flag.
   187	        if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_FS)
   188	            && clone_flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS | CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWUSER)
   189	        {
   190	            return_errno_with_message!(
   191	                Errno::EINVAL,
   192	                "`CLONE_FS` cannot be used together with `CLONE_NEWNS` or `CLONE_NEWUSER`"
   193	            );
   194	        }
   195	
   196	        // Reject invalid argument combinations related to the CLONE_PARENT flag.
   197	        if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT) {
   198	            if clone_flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWUSER | CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWPID) {
   199	                return_errno_with_message!(
   200	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   201	                    "`CLONE_PARENT` cannot be used together with `CLONE_NEWUSER` or `CLONE_NEWPID`"
   202	                );
   203	            }
   204	
   205	            if ctx.process.is_init_process() {
   206	                return_errno_with_message!(
   207	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   208	                    "`CLONE_PARENT` cannot be used if the process is the init process"
   209	                )
   210	            }
   211	        }
   212	
   213	        // Reject invalid argument combinations related to the CLONE_THREAD flag.
   214	        if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD) {
   215	            if !clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VM | CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND) {
   216	                return_errno_with_message!(
   217	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   218	                    "`CLONE_THREAD` without `CLONE_VM` and `CLONE_SIGHAND` is not valid"
   219	                );
   220	            }
   221	
   222	            if clone_flags.intersects(CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD | CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWUSER) {
   223	                return_errno_with_message!(
   224	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   225	                    "`CLONE_THREAD` cannot be used together with `CLONE_PIDFD` or `CLONE_NEWUSER`"
   226	                );
   227	            }
   228	        }
   229	
   230	        // Reject invalid argument combinations related to the CLONE_SIGHAND flag.
   231	        if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND)
   232	            && !clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VM)
   233	        {
   234	            return_errno_with_message!(
   235	                Errno::EINVAL,
   236	                "`CLONE_SIGHAND` without `CLONE_VM` is not valid"
   237	            );
   238	        }
   239	
   240	        Ok(())
   241	    }
   242	}
   243	
   244	impl From<u64> for CloneFlags {
   245	    fn from(flags: u64) -> Self {
   246	        // We use the lower 32 bits
   247	        let clone_flags = (flags & 0xffff_ffff) as u32;
   248	        CloneFlags::from_bits_truncate(clone_flags)
   249	    }
   250	}
   251	
   252	impl CloneFlags {
   253	    fn check_unsupported_flags(&self) -> Result<()> {
   254	        let supported_flags = CloneFlags::CLONE_VM
   255	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_FS
   256	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_FILES
   257	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_SIGHAND
   258	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_PIDFD
   259	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD
   260	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_SYSVSEM
   261	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_SETTLS
   262	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT_SETTID
   263	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_SETTID
   264	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID
   265	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK
   266	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS
   267	            | CloneFlags::CLONE_PARENT;
   268	        let unsupported_flags = *self - supported_flags;
   269	        if !unsupported_flags.is_empty() {
   270	            warn!("contains unsupported clone flags: {:?}", unsupported_flags);
   271	        }
   272	        Ok(())
   273	    }
   274	}
   275	
   276	/// Clone a child thread or child process.
   277	///
   278	/// FIXME: currently, the child process or thread will be scheduled to run at once,
   279	/// but this may not be the expected behavior.
   280	pub fn clone_child(
   281	    ctx: &Context,
   282	    parent_context: &UserContext,
   283	    clone_args: CloneArgs,
   284	) -> Result<Tid> {
   285	    clone_args.check(ctx)?;
   286	
   287	    if clone_args.flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_THREAD) {
   288	        let child_task = clone_child_task(ctx, parent_context, clone_args)?;
   289	        let child_thread = child_task.as_thread().unwrap();
   290	        child_thread.run();
   291	
   292	        let child_tid = child_thread.as_posix_thread().unwrap().tid();
   293	        Ok(child_tid)
   294	    } else {
   295	        let child_process = clone_child_process(ctx, parent_context, clone_args)?;
   296	        if clone_args.flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_VFORK) {
   297	            child_process.status().set_vfork_child(true);
   298	        }
   299	
   300	        child_process.run();
   301	
   302	        if child_process.status().is_vfork_child() {
   303	            let cond = || (!child_process.status().is_vfork_child()).then_some(());
   304	            let current = ctx.process.as_ref();
   305	            current.children_wait_queue().wait_until(cond);
   306	        }
   307	
   308	        let child_pid = child_process.pid();
   309	        FORKS_COUNTER
   310	            .get()
   311	            .unwrap()
   312	            .add_on_cpu(CpuId::current_racy(), 1);
   313	        Ok(child_pid)
   314	    }
   315	}
   316	
   317	fn clone_child_task(
   318	    ctx: &Context,
   319	    parent_context: &UserContext,
   320	    clone_args: CloneArgs,
   321	) -> Result<Arc<Task>> {
   322	    let clone_flags = clone_args.flags;
   323	
   324	    let Context {
   325	        process,
   326	        thread_local,
   327	        posix_thread,
   328	        ..
   329	    } = ctx;
   330	
   330	
   331	    // Clone system V semaphore
   332	    clone_sysvsem(clone_flags)?;
   333	
   334	    // Clone file table
   335	    let child_file_table = clone_files(thread_local.borrow_file_table().unwrap(), clone_flags);
   336	
   337	    // Clone fs
   338	    let child_fs = clone_fs(&thread_local.borrow_fs(), clone_flags);
   339	
   340	    // Clone FPU context
   341	    let child_fpu_context = thread_local.fpu().clone_context();
   342	
   343	    // Clone namespaces
   344	    let child_user_ns = thread_local.borrow_user_ns().clone();
   345	    let child_ns_proxy = clone_ns_proxy(
   346	        thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy().unwrap(),
   347	        &child_user_ns,
   348	        clone_flags,
   349	        posix_thread,
   350	    )?;
   351	
   352	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS) {
   353	        child_fs
   354	            .resolver()
   355	            .write()
   356	            .switch_to_mnt_ns(child_ns_proxy.mnt_ns())?;
   357	    }
   358	
   359	    let child_user_ctx = Box::new(clone_user_ctx(
   360	        parent_context,
   361	        clone_args.stack,
   362	        clone_args.stack_size,
   363	        clone_args.tls,
   364	        clone_flags,
   365	    ));
   366	
   367	    // Inherit sigmask from current thread
   368	    let sig_mask = posix_thread.sig_mask().load(Ordering::Relaxed).into();
   369	
   370	    // Inherit the thread name.
   371	    let thread_name = posix_thread.thread_name().lock().as_ref().cloned();
   372	
   373	    let child_tid = allocate_posix_tid();
   374	    let child_task = {
   375	        let credentials = {
   376	            let credentials = ctx.posix_thread.credentials();
   377	            Credentials::new_from(&credentials)
   378	        };
   379	
   380	        let mut thread_builder = PosixThreadBuilder::new(child_tid, child_user_ctx, credentials)
   381	            .process(posix_thread.weak_process())
   382	            .thread_name(thread_name)
   383	            .sig_mask(sig_mask)
   384	            .file_table(child_file_table)
   385	            .fs(child_fs)
   386	            .fpu_context(child_fpu_context)
   387	            .user_ns(child_user_ns)
   388	            .ns_proxy(child_ns_proxy);
   389	
   390	        // Deal with SETTID/CLEARTID flags
   391	        clone_parent_settid(child_tid, clone_args.parent_tid, clone_flags)?;
   392	        thread_builder = clone_child_cleartid(thread_builder, clone_args.child_tid, clone_flags);
   393	        thread_builder = clone_child_settid(thread_builder, clone_args.child_tid, clone_flags);
   394	
   395	        thread_builder.build()
   396	    };
   397	
   398	    process
   399	        .tasks()
   400	        .lock()
   401	        .insert(child_task.clone())
   402	        .map_err(|_| Error::with_message(Errno::EINTR, "the process has exited"))?;
   403	
   404	    Ok(child_task)
   405	}
   406	
   407	fn clone_child_process(
   408	    ctx: &Context,
   409	    parent_context: &UserContext,
   410	    clone_args: CloneArgs,
   411	) -> Result<Arc<Process>> {
   412	    let Context {
   413	        process,
   414	        thread_local,
   415	        posix_thread,
   416	        ..
   417	    } = ctx;
   418	
   419	    let clone_flags = clone_args.flags;
   420	
   421	    // Clone the virtual memory space
   422	    let child_process_vm = {
   423	        let parent_process_vm = process.vm();
   424	        clone_vm(parent_process_vm, clone_flags)?
   425	    };
   426	
   427	    // Clone the user context
   428	    let child_user_ctx = Box::new(clone_user_ctx(
   429	        parent_context,
   430	        clone_args.stack,
   431	        clone_args.stack_size,
   432	        clone_args.tls,
   433	        clone_flags,
   434	    ));
   435	
   436	    // Clone the file table
   437	    let child_file_table = clone_files(thread_local.borrow_file_table().unwrap(), clone_flags);
   438	
   439	    // Clone the filesystem information
   440	    let child_fs = clone_fs(&thread_local.borrow_fs(), clone_flags);
   441	
   442	    // Clone signal dispositions
   443	    let child_sig_dispositions = clone_sighand(process.sig_dispositions(), clone_flags);
   444	
   445	    // Clone System V semaphore
   446	    clone_sysvsem(clone_flags)?;
   447	
   448	    // Clone FPU context
   449	    let child_fpu_context = thread_local.fpu().clone_context();
   450	
   451	    // Clone the namespaces
   452	    let child_user_ns = clone_user_ns(clone_flags, thread_local)?;
   453	    let child_ns_proxy = clone_ns_proxy(
   454	        thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy().unwrap(),
   455	        &child_user_ns,
   456	        clone_flags,
   457	        posix_thread,
   458	    )?;
   459	
   460	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_NEWNS) {
   461	        child_fs
   462	            .resolver()
   463	            .write()
   464	            .switch_to_mnt_ns(child_ns_proxy.mnt_ns())?;
   465	    }
   466	
   467	    // Inherit the parent's signal mask
   468	    let child_sig_mask = posix_thread.sig_mask().load(Ordering::Relaxed).into();
   469	
   470	    // Inherit the parent's resource limits
   471	    let child_resource_limits = process.resource_limits().clone();
   472	
   473	    // Inherit the parent's nice value
   474	    let child_nice = process.nice().load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   475	
   476	    // Inherit the parent's OOM score adjustment
   477	    let child_oom_score_adj = process.oom_score_adj().load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   478	
   479	    let child_tid = allocate_posix_tid();
   480	
   481	    let child = {
   482	        let child_elf_path = process.executable_path();
   483	        let mut child_thread_builder = {
   484	            let child_thread_name = ThreadName::new_from_executable_path(&child_elf_path)?;
   485	
   486	            let credentials = {
   487	                let credentials = ctx.posix_thread.credentials();
   488	                Credentials::new_from(&credentials)
   489	            };
   490	
   491	            PosixThreadBuilder::new(child_tid, child_user_ctx, credentials)
   492	                .thread_name(Some(child_thread_name))
   493	                .sig_mask(child_sig_mask)
   494	                .file_table(child_file_table)
   495	                .fs(child_fs)
   496	                .fpu_context(child_fpu_context)
   497	                .user_ns(child_user_ns.clone())
   498	                .ns_proxy(child_ns_proxy)
   499	        };
   500	
   501	        // Deal with SETTID/CLEARTID flags
   502	        clone_parent_settid(child_tid, clone_args.parent_tid, clone_flags)?;
   503	        child_thread_builder =
   504	            clone_child_cleartid(child_thread_builder, clone_args.child_tid, clone_flags);
   505	        child_thread_builder =
   506	            clone_child_settid(child_thread_builder, clone_args.child_tid, clone_flags);
   507	
   508	        create_child_process(
   509	            child_tid,
   510	            &child_elf_path,
   511	            child_process_vm,
   512	            child_resource_limits,
   513	            child_nice,
   514	            child_oom_score_adj,
   515	            child_sig_dispositions,
   516	            child_user_ns,
   517	            child_thread_builder,
   518	        )
   519	    };
   520	
   521	    clone_pidfd(ctx, &child, clone_flags, clone_args.pidfd)?;
   522	
   523	    if let Some(sig) = clone_args.exit_signal {
   524	        child.set_exit_signal(sig);
   525	    };
   526	
   527	    // Sets parent process and group for child process.
   528	    set_parent_and_group(clone_flags, process, &child);
   529	
   530	    Ok(child)
   531	}
   532	
   533	fn clone_child_cleartid(
   534	    child_builder: PosixThreadBuilder,
   535	    child_tidptr: Vaddr,
   536	    clone_flags: CloneFlags,
   537	) -> PosixThreadBuilder {
   538	    if clone_flags.contains(CloneFlags::CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID) {
   539	        child_builder.clear_child_tid(child_tidptr)
   540	    } else {
   541	        child_builder
   542	    }
   543	}
   544	
   545	fn clone_child_settid(
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	//! Task sets.
     4	
     5	use ostd::task::{CurrentTask, Task};
     6	
     7	use crate::prelude::*;
     8	
     9	/// A task set that maintains all tasks in a POSIX process.
    10	pub struct TaskSet {
    11	    tasks: Vec<Arc<Task>>,
    12	    has_exited_main: bool,
    13	    has_exited_group: bool,
    14	}
    15	
    16	impl TaskSet {
    17	    /// Creates a new task set.
    18	    pub(super) fn new() -> Self {
    19	        Self {
    20	            tasks: Vec::new(),
    21	            has_exited_main: false,
    22	            has_exited_group: false,
    23	        }
    24	    }
    25	
    26	    /// Inserts a new task to the task set.
    27	    ///
    28	    /// This method will fail if [`Self::set_exited_group`] has been called before.
    29	    pub(super) fn insert(&mut self, task: Arc<Task>) -> core::result::Result<(), Arc<Task>> {
    30	        if self.has_exited_group {
    31	            return Err(task);
    32	        }
    33	
    34	        self.tasks.push(task);
    35	        Ok(())
    36	    }
    37	
    38	    /// Removes the exited task from the task set if necessary.
    39	    ///
    40	    /// The task will be removed from the task set if the corresponding thread is not the main
    41	    /// thread.
    42	    ///
    43	    /// This method will return true if there are no more alive tasks in the task set.
    44	    ///
    45	    /// # Panics
    46	    ///
    47	    /// This method will panic if the task is not in the task set.
    48	    pub(super) fn remove_exited(&mut self, task: &CurrentTask) -> bool {
    49	        let position = self
    50	            .tasks
    51	            .iter()
    52	            .position(|some_task| core::ptr::eq(some_task.as_ref(), task.as_ref()))
    53	            .unwrap();
    54	
    55	        if position == 0 {
    56	            assert!(!self.has_exited_main);
    57	            self.has_exited_main = true;
    58	        } else {
    59	            self.tasks.swap_remove(position);
    60	        }
    61	
    62	        self.has_exited_main && self.tasks.len() == 1
    63	    }
    64	
    65	    /// Sets a flag that denotes that an `exit_group` has been initiated.
    66	    pub(super) fn set_exited_group(&mut self) {
    67	        self.has_exited_group = true;
    68	    }
    69	
    70	    /// Returns whether an `exit_group` has been initiated.
    71	    pub(super) fn has_exited_group(&self) -> bool {
    72	        self.has_exited_group
    73	    }
    74	}
    75	
    76	impl TaskSet {
    77	    /// Returns a slice of the tasks in the task set.
    78	    pub fn as_slice(&self) -> &[Arc<Task>] {
    79	        self.tasks.as_slice()
    80	    }
    81	
    82	    /// Returns the main task/thread.
    83	    pub fn main(&self) -> &Arc<Task> {
    84	        &self.tasks[0]
    85	    }
    86	}

    82	///
    83	/// This function can only be called once and must be called during the initialization phase of kernel,
    84	/// before any [`Task`]-related APIs are invoked.
    85	pub fn inject_scheduler(scheduler: &'static dyn Scheduler<Task>) {
    86	    SCHEDULER.call_once(|| scheduler);

    89	/// Enables preemptive scheduling on the current CPU.
    90	///
    91	/// After calling this function on a CPU,
    92	/// a task that is executing in the user mode may get preempted
    93	/// if another runnable task on the same CPU is deemed more urgent by the scheduler.
    94	///
    95	/// OSTD achieves task preemption by registering a per-CPU timer callback
    96	/// to invoke the scheduler periodically.
    97	/// Thus, this function should be called _once_ on every CPU
    98	/// by an OSTD-based kernel during its initialization phase,
    99	/// after it has injected its scheduler via [`inject_scheduler`].
   100	pub fn enable_preemption_on_cpu() {
   101	    timer::register_callback_on_cpu(|| {
   102	        SCHEDULER.get().unwrap().mut_local_rq_with(&mut |local_rq| {
   103	            let should_pick_next = local_rq.update_current(UpdateFlags::Tick);
   104	            if should_pick_next {
   105	                cpu_local::set_need_preempt();
   106	            }
   107	        })
   108	    });
   109	}
   110	
   111	static SCHEDULER: Once<&'static dyn Scheduler<Task>> = Once::new();
   112	
   113	/// A SMP-aware task scheduler.
   114	pub trait Scheduler<T = Task>: Sync + Send {
   115	    /// Enqueues a runnable task.
   116	    ///
   117	    /// The scheduler implementer can perform load-balancing or some time accounting work here.
   118	    ///
   119	    /// The newly-enqueued task may have a higher priority than the currently running one on a CPU
   120	    /// and thus should preempt the latter.
   121	    /// In this case, this method returns the ID of that CPU.
   122	    fn enqueue(&self, runnable: Arc<T>, flags: EnqueueFlags) -> Option<CpuId>;
   123	
   124	    /// Gets an immutable access to the local runqueue of the current CPU.
   125	    fn local_rq_with(&self, f: &mut dyn FnMut(&dyn LocalRunQueue<T>));
   126	
   127	    /// Gets a mutable access to the local runqueue of the current CPU.
   128	    fn mut_local_rq_with(&self, f: &mut dyn FnMut(&mut dyn LocalRunQueue<T>));
   129	}
   130	
   131	/// A per-CPU, local runqueue.
   132	///
   133	/// This abstraction allows OSTD to inspect and manipulate local runqueues.
   134	///
   135	/// Conceptually, a local runqueue maintains:
   136	/// 1. A priority queue of runnable tasks.
   137	///    The definition of "priority" is left to the concrete implementation.
   138	/// 2. The current running task.
   139	///
   140	/// # Interactions with OSTD
   141	///
   142	/// ## Overview
   143	///
   144	/// It is crucial for implementers of `LocalRunQueue`
   145	/// to understand how OSTD interacts with local runqueues.
   146	///
   147	/// A local runqueue is consulted by OSTD in response to one of four scheduling events:
   148	/// - **Yielding**, triggered by [`Task::yield_now`], where the current task voluntarily gives up CPU time.
   149	/// - **Sleeping**, triggered by [`crate::sync::Waiter::wait`]
   150	///   or any synchronization primitive built upon it (e.g., [`crate::sync::WaitQueue`], [`crate::sync::Mutex`]),
   420	    let mut current = None;
   421	    let mut is_first_try = true;
   422	
   423	    reschedule(|local_rq: &mut dyn LocalRunQueue| {
   424	        let next_task_opt = if is_first_try {
   425	            if has_unparked() {
   426	                return ReschedAction::DoNothing;
   427	            }
   428	            is_first_try = false;
   429	
   430	            // Note the race conditions: the current task may be woken after the above `has_unparked`
   431	            // check, but before the below `dequeue_current` action, we need to make sure that the
   432	            // wakeup event isn't lost.
   433	            //
   434	            // Currently, for the FIFO and CFS scheduler, `Scheduler::enqueue` will try to lock `local_rq`
   435	            // when the above race condition occurs, so it will wait until we finish calling the
   436	            // `dequeue_current` method and nothing bad will happen. This may need to be revisited
   437	            // after more complex schedulers are introduced.
   438	
   439	            let should_pick_next = local_rq.update_current(UpdateFlags::Wait);
   440	            current = local_rq.dequeue_current();
   441	            should_pick_next.then(|| local_rq.pick_next())
   442	        } else {
   443	            local_rq.try_pick_next()
   444	        };
   445	
   446	        if let Some(next_task) = next_task_opt {
   447	            if Arc::ptr_eq(current.as_ref().unwrap(), next_task) {
   448	                // The current task has been woken and picked as the next runnable task.
   449	                return ReschedAction::DoNothing;
   450	            }
   451	            return ReschedAction::SwitchTo(next_task.clone());
   452	        }
   453	
   454	        ReschedAction::Retry
   455	    });
   456	}
   457	
   458	/// Unblocks a target task.
   459	pub(crate) fn unpark_target(runnable: Arc<Task>) {
   460	    let preempt_cpu = SCHEDULER
   461	        .get()
   462	        .unwrap()
   463	        .enqueue(runnable, EnqueueFlags::Wake);
   464	    if let Some(preempt_cpu_id) = preempt_cpu {
   465	        set_need_preempt(preempt_cpu_id);
   466	    }
   467	}

   491	fn set_need_preempt(cpu_id: CpuId) {
   492	    let preempt_guard = disable_preempt();
   493	
   494	    if preempt_guard.current_cpu() == cpu_id {
   495	        cpu_local::set_need_preempt();
   496	    } else {
   497	        crate::smp::inter_processor_call(&CpuSet::from(cpu_id), || {

   503	/// Dequeues the current task from its runqueue.
   504	///
   505	/// This should only be called if the current is to exit.
   506	#[track_caller]
   507	pub(super) fn exit_current() -> ! {
   508	    let mut is_first_try = true;
   509	
   510	    reschedule(|local_rq: &mut dyn LocalRunQueue| {
   511	        let next_task_opt = if is_first_try {
   512	            is_first_try = false;
   513	            let should_pick_next = local_rq.update_current(UpdateFlags::Exit);
   514	            let _current = local_rq.dequeue_current();
   515	            should_pick_next.then(|| local_rq.pick_next())
   516	        } else {
   517	            local_rq.try_pick_next()
   518	        };
   519	
   520	        if let Some(next_task) = next_task_opt {
   521	            ReschedAction::SwitchTo(next_task.clone())
   522	        } else {
   523	            ReschedAction::Retry
   524	        }
   525	    });
   526	
   527	    unreachable!()
   528	}
   529	
   530	/// Yields execution.
   531	#[track_caller]
   532	pub(super) fn yield_now() {
   533	    reschedule(|local_rq| {
   534	        let should_pick_next = local_rq.update_current(UpdateFlags::Yield);
   535	        let next_task_opt = should_pick_next.then(|| local_rq.pick_next());
   536	        if let Some(next_task) = next_task_opt {
   537	            ReschedAction::SwitchTo(next_task.clone())
   538	        } else {
   539	            ReschedAction::DoNothing
   540	        }
   541	    })
   542	}

   544	/// Do rescheduling by acting on the scheduling decision (`ReschedAction`) made by a
   545	/// user-given closure.
   546	///
   547	/// The closure makes the scheduling decision by taking the local runqueue has its input.
   548	#[track_caller]
   549	fn reschedule<F>(mut f: F)
   550	where
    35	
    36	    fn select_cpu(&self) -> CpuId {
    37	        // FIXME: adopt more reasonable policy once we fully enable SMP.
    38	        CpuId::bsp()
    39	    }

    41	
    42	impl<T: CommonSchedInfo + Send + Sync> Scheduler<T> for FifoScheduler<T> {
    43	    fn enqueue(&self, runnable: Arc<T>, flags: EnqueueFlags) -> Option<CpuId> {
    44	        let (still_in_rq, target_cpu) = {
    45	            let selected_cpu_id = self.select_cpu();
    46	
    47	            if let Err(task_cpu_id) = runnable.cpu().set_if_is_none(selected_cpu_id) {
    48	                debug_assert!(flags != EnqueueFlags::Spawn);
    49	                (true, task_cpu_id)
    50	            } else {
    51	                (false, selected_cpu_id)
    52	            }
    53	        };
    54	
    55	        let mut rq = self.rq[target_cpu.as_usize()].disable_irq().lock();
    56	        if still_in_rq && let Err(_) = runnable.cpu().set_if_is_none(target_cpu) {
    57	            return None;
    58	        }
    59	        rq.queue.push_back(runnable);
    60	
    61	        // All tasks are important. Do not preempt the current task without good reason.
    62	        None
    63	    }
    64	
    65	    fn local_rq_with(&self, f: &mut dyn FnMut(&dyn LocalRunQueue<T>)) {
    66	        let preempt_guard = disable_preempt();
    67	        let local_rq: &FifoRunQueue<T> = &self.rq[preempt_guard.current_cpu().as_usize()]
    68	            .disable_irq()
    69	            .lock();
    70	        f(local_rq);
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    fn mut_local_rq_with(&self, f: &mut dyn FnMut(&mut dyn LocalRunQueue<T>)) {
    74	        let preempt_guard = disable_preempt();
    75	        let local_rq: &mut FifoRunQueue<T> = &mut self.rq[preempt_guard.current_cpu().as_usize()]
    76	            .disable_irq()
    77	            .lock();
    78	        f(local_rq);
    79	    }
    80	}
    81	
    82	struct FifoRunQueue<T: CommonSchedInfo> {
    83	    current: Option<Arc<T>>,
    84	    queue: VecDeque<Arc<T>>,
    85	}

    87	impl<T: CommonSchedInfo> FifoRunQueue<T> {

   103	            UpdateFlags::Tick => false,
   104	            UpdateFlags::Wait | UpdateFlags::Yield | UpdateFlags::Exit => !self.queue.is_empty(),
   105	        }
   106	    }

   108	    fn try_pick_next(&mut self) -> Option<&Arc<T>> {
   109	        let next_task = self.queue.pop_front()?;
   110	        if let Some(prev_task) = self.current.replace(next_task) {
   111	            self.queue.push_back(prev_task);
   112	        }
   113	
   114	        self.current.as_ref()
   115	    }
   116	
   117	    fn dequeue_current(&mut self) -> Option<Arc<T>> {
   118	        self.current.take().inspect(|task| task.cpu().set_to_none())
   119	    }
   120	}
