# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 83

> ```diff
> /// If the `reaper_process` is zombie, returns `Err(())`.
> fn move_process_children(
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): The documentation says `Err(())` means `reaper_process` is zombie, but the implementation actually returns it when `reaper_process.children()` is `None`. The documented condition does not match the code's interface.

**Fix.** Document the actual condition, for example: `If the reaper's child list has already been cleared, returns Err(())`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 3

> ```diff
> //! Completely Fair Scheduler (CFS).
> ```

`module-docs` (minor): The module summary calls this `Completely Fair Scheduler (CFS)`, but the module owns `ClassScheduler` and the `stop`, `real_time`, `fair`, and `idle` scheduling classes. The description misleads readers about the module's responsibility.

**Fix.** Replace the summary with a description of scheduler-class dispatch, mentioning `ClassScheduler`, `SchedAttr`, and the per-class modules.
>     SpinLock::new(PerCpuClassRqSet {
> ```

`closure-fn-suffix` (nit): `class_rq` holds a closure, but its name reads like a run-queue value rather than a callable.

**Fix.** Rename the closure variable to `class_rq_fn` and use that name at `.map()`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 368

> ```diff
> SchedPolicyKind::Stop => (self.stop.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 0),
> SchedPolicyKind::RealTime => (self.real_time.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 1),
> SchedPolicyKind::Fair => (self.fair.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 2),
> SchedPolicyKind::Idle => (self.idle.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 3),
> ```

`no-magic-number` (minor): The `0`, `1`, `2`, and `3` values assigned to `lookahead`, followed by comparisons against `1` through `4`, encode the scheduler-class ordering without naming that invariant.

**Fix.** Replace the numeric ranks with named constants or a small ordering enum so the `lookahead` logic states which classes are eligible.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 36

> ```diff
> fn send_parent_death_signal(current_process: &Process) {
>     let current_children = current_process.children().lock();
>     for child in current_children.as_ref().unwrap().values() {
> ```

Incorrect parent-death timing (major): `send_parent_death_signal()` is reached only from `exit_process()`, which runs after the last thread in a process exits. If the thread that created a child exits while another thread in the same process remains alive, the child receives no `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` signal until the entire process exits.

**Fix.** Track the creating POSIX thread and deliver the parent-death signal from that thread's exit path, while preserving the required parent relationship and signal metadata.

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 43

> ```diff
> // FIXME: Set `si_pid` in the `siginfo_t` argument.
> let signal = KernelSignal::new(signum);
> child.enqueue_signal(signal);
> ```

Missing sender metadata (minor): The parent-death signal is created with `KernelSignal::new(signum)`, whose `siginfo_t` contains no sender PID. An `SA_SIGINFO` handler therefore receives a zero or unset `si_pid` instead of the exiting parent's PID.

**Fix.** Use a signal type that carries the parent PID and sets `siginfo_t.si_pid` before delivery, then add a regression test using an `SA_SIGINFO` handler.

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 102

> ```diff
> for child_process in current_children.as_mut().unwrap().values() {
>     let mut parent = child_process.parent.lock();
>     reaper_process_children.insert(child_process.pid(), child_process.clone());
>     parent.set_process(reaper_process);
> }
> ```

Lost wait wakeup (major): `move_process_children()` inserts children into `reaper_process.children()` but never wakes `reaper_process.children_wait_queue()`. If a child was already zombie and the reaper is blocked in `wait`, the old parent received the earlier notification and no later event wakes the reaper, so it can sleep indefinitely despite owning a waitable child.

**Fix.** After transferring the children, wake `reaper_process.children_wait_queue()`, at least when a transferred child is already zombie, and add a regression test with a blocked reaper and a pre-existing zombie child.

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 130

> ```diff
> let Some(parent) = current_process.parent().lock().process().upgrade() else {
>     return;
> };
> 
> if let Some(signal) = current_process.exit_signal().map(KernelSignal::new) {
>     parent.enqueue_signal(signal);
> };
> ```

Lost child-death notification (major): `exit_process()` marks the parent zombie before reparenting its children. If a child acquires `current_process.parent` after that status change but before `move_process_children()` updates the pointer, `parent.enqueue_signal()` returns immediately for the zombie parent; the child is then moved to the reaper without a `SIGCHLD` or wait-queue notification. A race where the signal is queued just before reparenting loses it when the old parent is discarded as well.

**Fix.** Synchronize child-exit notification with the parent-child handoff: revalidate the parent after acquiring the relevant children lock, retry after reparenting when the old parent is exiting, and notify the final parent rather than a zombie parent.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 180

> ```diff
> pub fn set_policy(&self, policy: SchedPolicy) {
>     self.policy.set(policy, |policy| match policy {
>         SchedPolicy::RealTime { rt_prio, rt_policy } => {
>             self.real_time.update(rt_prio.get(), rt_policy);
>         }
>         SchedPolicy::Fair(nice) => self.fair.update(nice),
>         _ => {}
>     });
> }
> ```

Stale scheduling class (major): `SchedAttr::set_policy()` and `SchedAttr::update_policy()` change scheduling attributes without reclassifying a task already stored in a run queue. A queued task changed from `SchedPolicy::Fair` to `SchedPolicy::RealTime` remains in `FairClassRq`, and a queued `SCHED_RR` task whose priority changes remains in its old `PrioArray` bucket.

**Fix.** Route policy changes through the scheduler and, under the owning run-queue lock, remove and re-enqueue runnable tasks using the new class and priority. Trigger rescheduling when the new policy outranks the current task, and add regression tests for queued class and priority changes.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 280

**Fix.** Route policy changes through the scheduler and, under the owning run-queue lock, remove and re-enqueue runnable tasks using the new class and priority. Trigger rescheduling when the new policy outranks the current task, and add regression tests for queued class and priority changes.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 280

> ```diff
> if let Some(last_cpu) = thread.sched_attr().last_cpu() {
>     return last_cpu;
> }
> debug_assert!(flags == EnqueueFlags::Spawn);
> ```

Affinity violation (major): `select_cpu()` returns `last_cpu` without checking the current affinity mask. A task that last ran on `CPU 0`, then blocks, has its affinity changed to only `CPU 1`, and is woken will still be enqueued on disallowed `CPU 0`.

**Fix.** Load the affinity mask before using `last_cpu`; reuse it only when the cached CPU is still allowed. Otherwise select an allowed CPU and coordinate any required migration or requeue with the affinity update.

## Security

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 102

> ```diff
> @@
> -        reaper_process_children.insert(child_process.pid(), child_process.clone());
>          parent.set_process(reaper_process);
> +        reaper_process_children.insert(child_process.pid(), child_process.clone());
> ```

Time-of-check/time-of-use (major): `child_process` is inserted into `reaper_process`'s children before its `parent` is updated. If that child concurrently calls `clone()` with `CLONE_PARENT`, it can observe the exiting process as its parent; `set_parent_and_group()` then waits forever on that process's permanently cleared `children` field, allowing an unprivileged process to pin a kernel thread in the syscall.

**Fix.** Update `child_process.parent` before publishing it in `reaper_process_children`, while holding both children locks, so concurrent `CLONE_PARENT` observes the new reaper.

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The review found two major scheduler correctness defects: policy changes do not reclassify queued tasks, and wakeups can reuse a cached CPU that is no longer in the task's affinity mask. The process-exit path also has confirmed notification and Linux-compatibility gaps around reparenting, `SIGCHLD`/wait wakeups, `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` timing, and `siginfo_t.si_pid`.
+
+The code does preserve an explicit children-lock ordering and retries when a selected reaper is exiting. The highest-priority fixes are to synchronize reparenting with notification delivery, requeue tasks when scheduling attributes change, and validate cached CPUs against current affinity.
 
 ## Maintainability
 
@@ -150,7 +152,7 @@
 
 Lost wait wakeup (major): `move_process_children()` inserts children into `reaper_process.children()` but never wakes `reaper_process.children_wait_queue()`. If a child was already zombie and the reaper is blocked in `wait`, the old parent received the earlier notification and no later event wakes the reaper, so it can sleep indefinitely despite owning a waitable child.
 
-**Fix.** After transferring the children, wake `reaper_process.children_wait_queue()`, at least when a transferred child is already zombie, and add a regression test with a blocked reaper and a pre-existing zombie child.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other reparenting-notification finding: synchronize child transfer and parent handoff with notification delivery, then wake the destination reaper's wait queue. Add a regression test with a pre-existing zombie child transferred to a blocked reaper.
 
 ### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 130
 
@@ -166,7 +168,7 @@
 
 Lost child-death notification (major): `exit_process()` marks the parent zombie before reparenting its children. If a child acquires `current_process.parent` after that status change but before `move_process_children()` updates the pointer, `parent.enqueue_signal()` returns immediately for the zombie parent; the child is then moved to the reaper without a `SIGCHLD` or wait-queue notification. A race where the signal is queued just before reparenting loses it when the old parent is discarded as well.
 
-**Fix.** Synchronize child-exit notification with the parent-child handoff: revalidate the parent after acquiring the relevant children lock, retry after reparenting when the old parent is exiting, and notify the final parent rather than a zombie parent.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other reparenting-notification finding: synchronize child transfer and parent handoff with notification delivery, then notify the final live parent and wake the destination reaper's wait queue. Add race tests covering a child exit concurrent with its parent's exit.

-> -        reaper_process_children.insert(child_process.pid(), child_process.clone());
->          parent.set_process(reaper_process);
-> +        reaper_process_children.insert(child_process.pid(), child_process.clone());
-> ```
+No confirmed findings after verification.
 
-Time-of-check/time-of-use (major): `child_process` is inserted into `reaper_process`'s children before its `parent` is updated. If that child concurrently calls `clone()` with `CLONE_PARENT`, it can observe the exiting process as its parent; `set_parent_and_group()` then waits forever on that process's permanently cleared `children` field, allowing an unprivileged process to pin a kernel thread in the syscall.
+## Retracted by verification
 
-**Fix.** Update `child_process.parent` before publishing it in `reaper_process_children`, while holding both children locks, so concurrent `CLONE_PARENT` observes the new reaper.
+- `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 102 (security): The claimed `CLONE_PARENT` hang is refuted because `move_process_children()` holds `current_process.children()` while updating each child's parent. A concurrent clone therefore either completes before the transfer or blocks until the list is cleared and retries; it cannot complete against a permanently stale parent.

# Summary

The review found two major scheduler correctness defects: policy changes do not reclassify queued tasks, and wakeups can reuse a cached CPU that is no longer in the task's affinity mask. The process-exit path also has confirmed notification and Linux-compatibility gaps around reparenting, `SIGCHLD`/wait wakeups, `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` timing, and `siginfo_t.si_pid`.

The code does preserve an explicit children-lock ordering and retries when a selected reaper is exiting. The highest-priority fixes are to synchronize reparenting with notification delivery, requeue tasks when scheduling attributes change, and validate cached CPUs against current affinity.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 83

> ```diff
> /// If the `reaper_process` is zombie, returns `Err(())`.
> fn move_process_children(
> ```

> ```diff
> if let Some(last_cpu) = thread.sched_attr().last_cpu() {
>     return last_cpu;
> }
> debug_assert!(flags == EnqueueFlags::Spawn);
> ```

Affinity violation (major): `select_cpu()` returns `last_cpu` without checking the current affinity mask. A task that last ran on `CPU 0`, then blocks, has its affinity changed to only `CPU 1`, and is woken will still be enqueued on disallowed `CPU 0`.

**Fix.** Load the affinity mask before using `last_cpu`; reuse it only when the cached CPU is still allowed. Otherwise select an allowed CPU and coordinate any required migration or requeue with the affinity update.

## Security

No confirmed findings after verification.

## Retracted by verification

- `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 102 (security): The claimed `CLONE_PARENT` hang is refuted because `move_process_children()` holds `current_process.children()` while updating each child's parent. A concurrent clone therefore either completes before the transfer or blocks until the list is cleared and retries; it cannot complete against a permanently stale parent.

 ## Maintainability
 
@@ -150,7 +152,7 @@
 
 Lost wait wakeup (major): `move_process_children()` inserts children into `reaper_process.children()` but never wakes `reaper_process.children_wait_queue()`. If a child was already zombie and the reaper is blocked in `wait`, the old parent received the earlier notification and no later event wakes the reaper, so it can sleep indefinitely despite owning a waitable child.
 
-**Fix.** After transferring the children, wake `reaper_process.children_wait_queue()`, at least when a transferred child is already zombie, and add a regression test with a blocked reaper and a pre-existing zombie child.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other reparenting-notification finding: synchronize child transfer and parent handoff with notification delivery, then wake the destination reaper's wait queue. Add a regression test with a pre-existing zombie child transferred to a blocked reaper.
 
 ### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 130
 
@@ -166,7 +168,7 @@
 
 Lost child-death notification (major): `exit_process()` marks the parent zombie before reparenting its children. If a child acquires `current_process.parent` after that status change but before `move_process_children()` updates the pointer, `parent.enqueue_signal()` returns immediately for the zombie parent; the child is then moved to the reaper without a `SIGCHLD` or wait-queue notification. A race where the signal is queued just before reparenting loses it when the old parent is discarded as well.
 
-**Fix.** Synchronize child-exit notification with the parent-child handoff: revalidate the parent after acquiring the relevant children lock, retry after reparenting when the old parent is exiting, and notify the final parent rather than a zombie parent.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other reparenting-notification finding: synchronize child transfer and parent handoff with notification delivery, then notify the final live parent and wake the destination reaper's wait queue. Add race tests covering a child exit concurrent with its parent's exit.

===== EXPECTED DEFECTS =====
# Expected defects

1. location: kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs lines 65-67 (persona: development, grounding: lock run queues with local IRQs disabled, severity: major)
   defect: `ClassScheduler::rqs` is a plain `SpinLock<PerCpuClassRqSet>`, so callers can lock a run queue without the type system requiring local IRQs to be disabled. The scheduler installs preemption callbacks with `enable_preemption_on_cpu`, and those callbacks can access the same run queues from interrupt context. If task context holds the run queue lock with IRQs enabled and the local interrupt callback tries to take it again, the CPU can deadlock.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should identify that per-CPU run queues are shared between task and interrupt/preemption callback contexts, and require the lock to enforce local IRQ disabling for every acquisition, not just selected call sites.

2. location: kernel/src/process/exit.rs lines 116-126 (persona: development, grounding: wake waiters after reparenting children, severity: major)
   defect: `move_children_to_reaper_process` moves all children from an exiting process to a subreaper, but it returns without waking the new parent's `children_wait_queue`. A process already blocked in `wait*()` sleeps on that queue until a child status change wakes it; if dead children were just reparented to it, the waiter may never re-check its child list and reap them. This can leave LTP `kill08`-style waits stuck forever.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag that reparenting creates newly waitable children for the reaper/init process and must wake that process's child wait queue on both successful paths.

# Summary

The review found two major scheduler correctness defects: policy changes do not reclassify queued tasks, and wakeups can reuse a cached CPU that is no longer in the task's affinity mask. The process-exit path also has confirmed notification and Linux-compatibility gaps around reparenting, `SIGCHLD`/wait wakeups, `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` timing, and `siginfo_t.si_pid`.

The code does preserve an explicit children-lock ordering and retries when a selected reaper is exiting. The highest-priority fixes are to synchronize reparenting with notification delivery, requeue tasks when scheduling attributes change, and validate cached CPUs against current affinity.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 83

> ```diff
> /// If the `reaper_process` is zombie, returns `Err(())`.
> fn move_process_children(
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): The documentation says `Err(())` means `reaper_process` is zombie, but the implementation actually returns it when `reaper_process.children()` is `None`. The documented condition does not match the code's interface.

**Fix.** Document the actual condition, for example: `If the reaper's child list has already been cleared, returns Err(())`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 3

> ```diff
> //! Completely Fair Scheduler (CFS).
> ```

`module-docs` (minor): The module summary calls this `Completely Fair Scheduler (CFS)`, but the module owns `ClassScheduler` and the `stop`, `real_time`, `fair`, and `idle` scheduling classes. The description misleads readers about the module's responsibility.

**Fix.** Replace the summary with a description of scheduler-class dispatch, mentioning `ClassScheduler`, `SchedAttr`, and the per-class modules.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 10

> ```diff
> use ostd::{
>     arch::read_tsc as sched_clock,
>     cpu::{all_cpus, CpuId, PinCurrentCpu},
>     irq::disable_local,
> ```

`qualified-fn-imports` (minor): Free functions such as `all_cpus`, `disable_local`, `enable_preemption_on_cpu`, `inject_scheduler`, and `set_stats_from_scheduler` are imported directly, so call sites hide which module owns each operation.

**Fix.** Import the parent modules and call these functions through qualified paths such as `cpu::all_cpus()`, `irq::disable_local()`, `scheduler::inject_scheduler()`, and `stats::set_stats_from_scheduler()`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 62

> ```diff
> /// Represents the middle layer between scheduling classes and generic scheduler
> /// traits. It consists of all the sets of run queues for CPU cores. Other global
> /// information may also be stored here.
> pub struct ClassScheduler {
> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (nit): The type summaries for `ClassScheduler`, `PerCpuClassRqSet`, and `CurrentRuntime` begin with action verbs such as `Represents` and `Stores` instead of noun phrases, and the `CurrentRuntime` explanation contains the malformed phrase `instead encapsulating`.

**Fix.** Rewrite the summaries as concise noun phrases and clarify the rationale, for example `Runtime accounting for the currently running task` and `This state is kept separately from the task because scheduling classes only consume runtime and attributes.`

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 65

> ```diff
> pub struct ClassScheduler {
>     rqs: Box<[SpinLock<PerCpuClassRqSet>]>,
>     last_chosen_cpu: AtomicCpuId,
> }
> 
> impl ClassScheduler {
>     pub fn new() -> Self {
> ```

`narrow-visibility` (minor): `ClassScheduler` and its `new` constructor are public even though the `sched_class` module is private and the type is only constructed internally by `init()`. This exposes implementation surface without an actual consumer.

**Fix.** Make `ClassScheduler` and `ClassScheduler::new` private; retain only the public entry points and attributes that have external consumers.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 180

> ```diff
> pub fn set_policy({prompt}self, policy: SchedPolicy) {
>     self.policy.set(policy, |policy| match policy {
>         SchedPolicy::RealTime { rt_prio, rt_policy } => {
> ```

`dry` (minor): `set_policy` and `update_policy` duplicate the same policy-to-class-attribute synchronization match. Adding or changing a scheduling class now requires editing both paths, which can leave them inconsistent.

**Fix.** Extract the shared match into a private helper and call it from both `set_policy` and `update_policy`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 263

> ```diff
> let class_rq = |cpu| {
>     SpinLock::new(PerCpuClassRqSet {
> ```

`closure-fn-suffix` (nit): `class_rq` holds a closure, but its name reads like a run-queue value rather than a callable.

**Fix.** Rename the closure variable to `class_rq_fn` and use that name at `.map()`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 368

> ```diff
> SchedPolicyKind::Stop => (self.stop.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 0),
> SchedPolicyKind::RealTime => (self.real_time.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 1),
> SchedPolicyKind::Fair => (self.fair.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 2),
> SchedPolicyKind::Idle => (self.idle.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 3),
> ```

`no-magic-number` (minor): The `0`, `1`, `2`, and `3` values assigned to `lookahead`, followed by comparisons against `1` through `4`, encode the scheduler-class ordering without naming that invariant.

**Fix.** Replace the numeric ranks with named constants or a small ordering enum so the `lookahead` logic states which classes are eligible.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 36

> ```diff
> fn send_parent_death_signal(current_process: {prompt}Process) {
>     let current_children = current_process.children().lock();
>     for child in current_children.as_ref().unwrap().values() {
> ```

Incorrect parent-death timing (major): `send_parent_death_signal()` is reached only from `exit_process()`, which runs after the last thread in a process exits. If the thread that created a child exits while another thread in the same process remains alive, the child receives no `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` signal until the entire process exits.

**Fix.** Track the creating POSIX thread and deliver the parent-death signal from that thread's exit path, while preserving the required parent relationship and signal metadata.

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 43

> ```diff
> // FIXME: Set `si_pid` in the `siginfo_t` argument.
> let signal = KernelSignal::new(signum);
> child.enqueue_signal(signal);
> ```

Missing sender metadata (minor): The parent-death signal is created with `KernelSignal::new(signum)`, whose `siginfo_t` contains no sender PID. An `SA_SIGINFO` handler therefore receives a zero or unset `si_pid` instead of the exiting parent's PID.

**Fix.** Use a signal type that carries the parent PID and sets `siginfo_t.si_pid` before delivery, then add a regression test using an `SA_SIGINFO` handler.

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 102

> ```diff
> for child_process in current_children.as_mut().unwrap().values() {
>     let mut parent = child_process.parent.lock();
>     reaper_process_children.insert(child_process.pid(), child_process.clone());
>     parent.set_process(reaper_process);
> }
> ```

Lost wait wakeup (major): `move_process_children()` inserts children into `reaper_process.children()` but never wakes `reaper_process.children_wait_queue()`. If a child was already zombie and the reaper is blocked in `wait`, the old parent received the earlier notification and no later event wakes the reaper, so it can sleep indefinitely despite owning a waitable child.

**Fix.** Shared with the other reparenting-notification finding: synchronize child transfer and parent handoff with notification delivery, then wake the destination reaper's wait queue. Add a regression test with a pre-existing zombie child transferred to a blocked reaper.

### `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 130

> ```diff
> let Some(parent) = current_process.parent().lock().process().upgrade() else {
>     return;
> };
> 
> if let Some(signal) = current_process.exit_signal().map(KernelSignal::new) {
>     parent.enqueue_signal(signal);
> };
> ```

Lost child-death notification (major): `exit_process()` marks the parent zombie before reparenting its children. If a child acquires `current_process.parent` after that status change but before `move_process_children()` updates the pointer, `parent.enqueue_signal()` returns immediately for the zombie parent; the child is then moved to the reaper without a `SIGCHLD` or wait-queue notification. A race where the signal is queued just before reparenting loses it when the old parent is discarded as well.

**Fix.** Shared with the other reparenting-notification finding: synchronize child transfer and parent handoff with notification delivery, then notify the final live parent and wake the destination reaper's wait queue. Add race tests covering a child exit concurrent with its parent's exit.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 180

> ```diff
> pub fn set_policy({prompt}self, policy: SchedPolicy) {
>     self.policy.set(policy, |policy| match policy {
>         SchedPolicy::RealTime { rt_prio, rt_policy } => {
>             self.real_time.update(rt_prio.get(), rt_policy);
>         }
>         SchedPolicy::Fair(nice) => self.fair.update(nice),
>         _ => {}
>     });
> }
> ```

Stale scheduling class (major): `SchedAttr::set_policy()` and `SchedAttr::update_policy()` change scheduling attributes without reclassifying a task already stored in a run queue. A queued task changed from `SchedPolicy::Fair` to `SchedPolicy::RealTime` remains in `FairClassRq`, and a queued `SCHED_RR` task whose priority changes remains in its old `PrioArray` bucket.

**Fix.** Route policy changes through the scheduler and, under the owning run-queue lock, remove and re-enqueue runnable tasks using the new class and priority. Trigger rescheduling when the new policy outranks the current task, and add regression tests for queued class and priority changes.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/mod.rs` line 280

> ```diff
> if let Some(last_cpu) = thread.sched_attr().last_cpu() {
>     return last_cpu;
> }
> debug_assert!(flags == EnqueueFlags::Spawn);
> ```

Affinity violation (major): `select_cpu()` returns `last_cpu` without checking the current affinity mask. A task that last ran on `CPU 0`, then blocks, has its affinity changed to only `CPU 1`, and is woken will still be enqueued on disallowed `CPU 0`.

**Fix.** Load the affinity mask before using `last_cpu`; reuse it only when the cached CPU is still allowed. Otherwise select an allowed CPU and coordinate any required migration or requeue with the affinity update.

No confirmed findings after verification.

- `kernel/src/process/exit.rs` line 102 (security): The claimed `CLONE_PARENT` hang is refuted because `move_process_children()` holds `current_process.children()` while updating each child's parent. A concurrent clone therefore either completes before the transfer or blocks until the list is cleared and retries; it cannot complete against a permanently stale parent.
